ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u121	Thank you for your word. Say good-bye again! The Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy is held at its 37th session. 5. 2017 as a parent working body, considered the proposal for the adoption of an authentic interpretation of Article 33(3) of the Act on measures taken by the Republic of Slovenia to strengthen the stability of banks submitted to the National Assembly by the Government. In its opening remarks, on behalf of the applicant, the State Secretary of the Ministry of Finance gave reasons for the proposal for the adoption of an authentic interpretation and explained that the purpose of the proposal is to remedy the confusion with regard to Article 33(3) of the Law on Measures taken by the Republic of Slovenia to strengthen the stability of banks. The Legislative and Legal Service presented its findings in the written opinion and stated that the National Assembly shall adopt an authentic explanation when the legal standard is unclear. The Service pointed out that, in order to understand the provision properly, a language interpretation was sufficient, since the text of the provision could already understand its proper meaning. The department doesn't think there's any need for an authentic explanation. With regard to the admissibility of the authentic interpretation, however, the service has no comments, since the authentic interpretation does not extend or alter the meaning of the legal norm. Following an introductory explanation by the representatives of the Government and the Legal and Legislative Service, the Committee has moved on to consider whether the proposed authentic interpretation of the law should be adopted. Following the discussion, the Committee moved to a decision and adopted a decision: The Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy considers that an authentic interpretation of Article 33(3) of the Act on measures taken by the Republic of Slovenia to strengthen the stability of banks is necessary. The Committee adopted that decision by 14 for and no one opposed. On the basis of the decision adopted, the Committee went on to examine the text of the authentic interpretation of the law. On the basis of Article 150(3) of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee also adopted its amendment proposed to the National Assembly for adoption. Since the Committee had already carried out its substantive discussion on the text of the authentic interpretation proposal in the course of the discussion on the above-mentioned proposal for a decision, it also immediately proceeded to the vote on the following proposal for a decision: the Finance and Monetary Policy Committee proposes to the National Assembly to adopt a proposal for the adoption of an authentic interpretation of Article 33 of the Act on measures taken by the Republic of Slovenia to strengthen the stability of banks and the amendment of the Committee amending the entire text of the authentic interpretation. The Committee adopted the above with 14 for and would not object. Thank you.

The Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy unanimously adopted the conclusion that an authentic declaration is necessary, an amendment leaving a reference to the law governing offences, since retroactivity in criminal offences is not permissible, and an authentic interpretation that reads as follows: “Article 33(3) of the ZUKSB should be understood to mean that the limitation periods for compensation claims arising prior to the entry into force of this Act have not yet expired, a quadrupling of the general limitation periods laid down by the law governing the bond relations.” By adopting this authentic interpretation, the National Assembly would finally and nevertheless adopt a completely clear, understandable and completely unambiguous interpretation of Article 33, paragraph 3, of the ZUKSB, and we will therefore support the proposal of an authentic interpretation and an amendment in the modern Centre's Group of the Parties.

The draft law was tabled by the Government for consideration by the National Assembly. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the Minister of Magi. Goran Klemenčich.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u141	Dear Vice-President of the National Assembly, thank you for your input. Dear Members and Members! The applicable Mutuality Act was adopted in February 1999. This is a law governing the method and procedure of determining reciprocity as a special condition for the acquisition of the property rights of foreigners in properties in the Republic of Slovenia. Since the adoption of a law that has not changed significantly in all this time, a number of things have happened. Article 68 of the Constitution has changed, Slovenia has acceded to certain international integrations and has concluded several EU, OECD and so on. As a result of these changes, the Ministry of Justice, under this law, currently generally recognises reciprocity only in cases where property rights are acquired by nationals of EU candidate countries. Earlier this, according to the domestic statement, was all foreigners, today the law is limited only to the citizens of Macedonia, Montenegro and Serbia. The amended regulation on both the rights of foreigners to acquire property rights in our country and the experience after the long implementation of the law has shown that there are a number of unnecessary administrative obstacles to the law adopted in 1999 that this is no longer adapted to the changed conditions of Slovenia's integration into the European Union and amended Article 68 of the Constitution and the accession to the OECD. And that dictated the adoption of a new law. The draft law thus amends the amended provisions of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia and other international treaties to which Slovenia has acceded. Unnecessary administrative burdens are eliminated and legal procedures for determining reciprocity are streamlined. So much for a little while. Since the conditions are met, I propose to the National Assembly that it also carry out a third examination of the law in this reference. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u143	Thank you very much for your word, Madam President. Dear fellows! Ms Tina Brecelj, representative of the proposer and secretary of state at the Ministry of Justice, said in an additional explanation of the articles that the 1999 Mutuality Act in force had survived, as the current regulation on the rights of foreigners to acquire property rights on immovable property in the Republic of Slovenia had changed considerably from its application to today. Thus, the proposal represents a harmonisation of the current regime with other rules, a removal of administrative burdens and a rationalisation of the reciprocity procedure, which will allow for faster and easier enforcement of the rights of the parties. It also no longer lays down the Government's reporting obligations to the National Assembly, and the record of decisions issued will be published on the websites of the Ministry of Justice. ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u144	The following is a presentation of the views of the groups of Members. The word is given by the Group of the Democratic Party of the Pensioners of Slovenia, for which Mrs Marjana Kotnik Poropat has spoken.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u145	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Dear Members and Members of Parliament, Minister of Respect! The 1999 Mutuality Act has survived since the current regime on the rights of foreigners to acquire property rights on immovable property in the Republic of Slovenia has changed considerably from its application to today. On the basis of the provisions currently in force, it is no longer possible to draw up a report containing absolute information on the number of foreigners who have requested and acquired property rights in Slovenia. The current law also contains a number of obstacles that would make the procedure under the law of reciprocity faster and more effective. The proposal for a law shall be adapted to the amended decision of the Constitutional Court concerning the acquisition of property rights on immovable property and shall comply with other relevant provisions, the Real Law and the Land Book Act. In addition, it removes a number of administrative burdens and rationalises the process. The party in the proceedings will therefore no longer be obliged to attach a land register, the Ministry of Justice, as the contractor of the reciprocity procedure, will obtain it only after the official duty. Nor will a foreigner be required to declare for what purposes he acquires property, nor is the submission of the document of the Geodetic Authority of the Republic of Slovenia envisaged. The reporting to the National Assembly is suspended twice a year, as these reports are not integrated, but will be published twice a year in the future Ministry of Justice on its website on what the results were. With a view to shortening the procedure, the law no longer provides for a complaint against the decisions but only an administrative dispute. In the Group of Members of the Democratic Party of Pensioners of Slovenia, we support adaptation to the changes and rationalisation of the regime, so we will support the proposal of the law. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u146	I give my word to the New Slovenia Group – Christian Democrats, for which Mr Jožef Horvat.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u147	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Dear Mr. Secretary, dear colleagues and colleagues! The Mutuality Act, which is before us, governs the method and procedure for determining reciprocity as a condition for the acquisition of property rights of foreigners and foreign countries on property in Slovenia. Reciprocality will still need to be determined for each property separately under the new law, and the so-called principle of material reciprocity will still apply. In accordance with this principle, a Slovenian national in a foreign country may acquire property rights on property under the same or similar conditions as that for the acquisition of property rights on property in Slovenia. The law brings the provisions into line with Article 68 of the Slovenian Constitution. Foreigners from certain countries may have the same status as Slovenian nationals in accordance with these constitutional provisions in the acquisition of property rights on immovable property, but only if the basis is the law or international treaties. In these cases, the condition of reciprocity does not need to be established. Since this is a case of compliance with constitutional provisions, we do not oppose these changes by Christian Democrats. With regard to the remedy against the decision, a regulation is maintained whereby there is no appeal against the decision and an administrative dispute is possible. Any other arrangement would be inadmissible and contrary to the constitutional order. However, unlike the regulation in force, it is no longer envisaged that, in an administrative dispute, the State Prosecutor would be able to bring an action. Given that the position and the role of the Attorney General falls within the area of criminal law and has the opportunity to raise an administrative dispute already in the municipality and, on the other hand, the Attorney General or the new Attorney of State, we do not object to this amendment. The bill also brings a novelty according to which reciprocity does not need to be determined in the event of the acquisition of property rights on the part of the property if the foreigner in the land book is already registered as a co-owner or joint owner on that property. This change brings meaningful simplification and prevention of duplication of administrative procedures, so we do not oppose it in Novi Slovenija. A key novelty is a simplification of the regime when the reciprocity of a foreign country has already been established in the previous case and it is a matter of obtaining ownership of the same type of property in the same area and of equal legal activity. In such a case, the Ministry shall issue a decision establishing reciprocity within thirty days of receipt of the full application to the party, which shall contain only a reduced explanation. In this case, too, we see a meaningful execution of the duplicates of the entire proceedings. If reciprocity for the same type of property in the same area has already been established and it is also an equivalent legal business, we also find it pointless to require long-term procedures to be conducted in full. Having gained experience with the proposed arrangement, it may also be appropriate to consider an even more simplified model of finding reciprocity in such cases. In Novi Slovenije we will mainly support the proposal for a law on mutuality due to administrative simplification.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u148	The word shall be given by the Parliamentary Group of the Modern Centre Party, Mrs Teja Ljubič.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u149	Mr President, thank you for the floor. Dear Members and Members of Parliament, Minister, a very nice greeting to all! Since the adoption of the current reciprocity law, almost 20 years have passed. The application of this law then laid down a type of reciprocity which was a condition for the acquisition of the property rights of foreigners on properties in Slovenia laid down in Article 68 of the Constitution, and the procedure and competence to determine it was also regulated by such adoption of the law. However, since almost 20 years is certainly a long time, many regulations have changed during this time, which have had a significant impact on the regulation of the rights of foreigners so that they can acquire property rights in our country. Changes in the normative field are necessary both because of the experience and observations that arise in the implementation of the provisions of this Act itself, as well as because of the harmonisation of the law with other regulations, and many reasons also stem from the rationalisation of the reciprocity process itself and the removal of the administrative barriers created by the legislation in force. Today, citizens of the EU, EEA and OECD Member States may acquire real estate in the territory of Slovenia on the basis of all legal bases under the same conditions as nationals of Slovenia, and in order to acquire property rights in properties in the Republic of Slovenia they do not need a decision to establish reciprocity. On the basis of the list of decisions issued by the Ministry of Justice, it is not possible to determine the number of foreigners who become owners of property in Slovenia, nor their nationality. Such a review is significantly clearer in the report on recorded traffic and property prices in border administrative units. This is also a fundamental reason why the current reporting to the National Assembly twice a year by the Government or the Ministry of Justice no longer makes sense. After the proposed amendment, the suspension of the regular reporting of the Ministry of Justice will nevertheless at any time require the preparation of a special report. It will also provide public insight into the situation, as the Ministry will publish statistics on certificates and decisions issued every six months on its website. Reciprocality is also maintained after the proposed solutions for certain cases, and the essential novelty of the proposed solutions is to simplify the regime in cases where the acquisition of property rights based on legal business is concerned and it will be concluded that, with regard to the same type of real estate lying in an equivalent area and as regards the type of legal business, as dealt with in a particular case, it has already been found that there is reciprocity for a foreign country. In such a case, the Ministry shall issue a decision establishing reciprocity within 30 days of receipt of the full application to the party, which shall contain only a reduced explanation. The proposal for a law on mutuality certainly enjoys the support of Members and Members of the Modern Centre Party. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u169	Thank you, President for your word. Dear Madam Secretary of State, colleagues, colleagues, nice greeting! The Committee on Agriculture, Forestry and Food considered the proposed Act on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on the Agricultural Forestry Chamber of Slovenia, submitted by Mr Andrej Čuš, as a working body at the 19th meeting on 4 May. The Committee was given an opinion by the Legislative and Legal Service, which made specific comments on Articles 1, 2 and 3 of the draft law. In addition, the representative of the Legislative and Legal Service pointed out in her supplementary explanation of the written opinion that the replacement of the current mandatory membership in the Chamber of Voluntary Members into the legal regime introduces a substantial conceptual shift, since the obligation or voluntaryness of the mission represents one of the dark elements of the definition of the system of assembly. Such an important substantive change always requires a comprehensive assessment and consistent legislative implementation, which would be more appropriate in the new law. A representative of the Commission of the State Council for Agriculture, Forestry and Food and the Agricultural Forestry Chamber of Slovenia also participated in the discussion, explaining the reasons why neither the proposals of the Law nor the Chamber nor the Commission support them. The fact is that the Agricultural Forestry Chamber is established by law and that membership is compulsory and a membership to which the Government agrees is paid, which gives the Chamber representativeness. The abolition of compulsory membership would result in a drastic decline in membership, so it would be difficult for the Chamber to remain a representative organisation of farmers. In addition, due to the adoption of previous amendments to the House Law, the number of members decreased by 60,000 and thus also to membership fees. The abolition of compulsory membership would also mean that the Chamber would become a representative of only large farms and no one would represent the interests of small farmers who need the most assistance. In view of the rejection of the Government's opinion and the comments of the Legislative and Legal Service, I myself, as Chairman of the Committee, have proposed that, in accordance with the second paragraph of Article 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, a proposal for a decision should not be considered as suitable for further consideration, in order to allow both the proposer and the parliamentary groups to present their views at the meeting of the National Assembly itself and that the law may also be voted on. With regard to the proposed decision, some members of the Committee stressed that the proposal for a law on the grounds of defective, incomplete and inadequate solutions is indeed not appropriate for further consideration, but that it is appropriate for the Government and the Chamber to consider the urgent reorganisation of the Chamber. The proposed amendment raises an important question of how to reorganise the assembly system in the future, in order to better follow the needs of small farmers in particular, to participate more actively in increasing self-sustainment and reducing the growth of agricultural land. They also asked why the Chamber does not have an organised forestry service, although forestry is mentioned in the title of the Chamber, whose presence on the ground would be urgently needed. These and other issues will need to be opened as soon as possible, and the initiator of these discussions should be quite the chamber itself. After the discussion, the Committee adopted the following conclusion: "The proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on the Agricultural Forestry Chamber is not suitable for further consideration." Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u172	For the opening presentation of the position on the proposal of the parent working body, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, State Secretary at the Ministry of Agriculture, Forestry and Food mags. Tanji Strniša.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u173	Mr President, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! As presented, the aim of the proposed proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Law on the Agricultural Forestry Chamber of Slovenia is to abolish the current mandatory membership. The government considers that, by introducing such a change in law and by introducing voluntary membership, the status of the Slovenian Agricultural Forestry Chamber would change from a public-law to a private-law chamber into an interesting association of those who would pay membership fees. The applicable law defines the position of the Agricultural Forestry Chamber, which is a person of public law who has public powers to decide on the rights and obligations of members. Compulsory membership, as well as the obligation to pay membership fees, the amount of which is under the control of the State, are legally defined. It follows from the current legal and system regime that the Chamber is a representative body representing the interests of those engaged in agricultural or forestry activities in the broadest sense. The Agricultural Forestry Chamber of Slovenia represents its members and also cooperates with its institutions with an expert opinion on the procedures for regulatory regulation of the economic system, in particular the area of agriculture, forestry and rural development. The Government further considers that the statutory membership and the obligation to pay the membership fee ensure the number of memberships and thus the representativeness of the Chamber. The number of members in the proposed amendment would be drastically reduced and the representativeness, independence and professional functioning of the Chamber would certainly be compromised. The second fact is that the membership resulting from compulsory membership has the character of a public benefit. If the membership of the Chamber were voluntary, the level of membership would also be a matter of agreement between the members, and thus the level of membership should be in proportion to the extent of the activity of the Chamber for which membership is compulsory, and the level of this contribution should be given by the Government on an annual basis. In the light of the above, in the opinion of the Government, the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on the Agricultural Forestry Chamber of Slovenia is not suitable for further consideration. Thank you very much.

In this situation as it is, we consider this to be a more appropriate form than the immediate abolition of compulsory membership. One of the important dilemmas we had at the time of the establishment of the Agricultural Forestry Chamber was, after all, the status of public institutions, agricultural and agricultural institutions, which, of course, came under the auspices of the Agricultural Forestry Chamber. This was a very serious transition and, in fact, sometimes this relationship is being distorted, because, of course, when this transfer was carried out, the State budget obligation to finance these institutions was also assumed. ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u183	Thank you, President, for your word. Madam Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues! The Committee on Agriculture, Forestry and Food considered the proposal for a law amending the Law on Agricultural Lands, submitted to the National Assembly by Mr Andrej Chuš, as a working body at its 19th meeting, on 4 May this year. In accordance with Article 145 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the draft law was sent to the local communities. Two opinions were sent to the Committee, namely the opinion of the Municipality of Ljubljana, which emphasises that the proposed partial solution to the Agricultural Lands Act in the chapter on Agriculture Transport would indeed improve the position of young farmers in improving the possibility of purchasing agricultural land, but they estimate that it would be better to fully open and redefine the chapter on agricultural land transport. The opinion of the Community of the municipalities of Slovenia has also been forwarded, which emphasises that the proposal for a law partially addresses or improves the situation of young farmers, which they do not object to, but considers that it is necessary to seek integrated solutions while improving the situation of local communities by including pre-purchase beneficiaries in the purchase of agricultural land. Accordingly, the Community of the municipalities of Slovenia proposes that in that Act Chapter 3, the transport of agricultural land, forests and farms be fully opened and redefined. At the time of presentation of their written opinion, the representative of the Legislative and Legal Service reiterated that due to the clear and unequivocal determination of the entity, the concept of young farmers should be defined at the legal level, taking into account the content of the regulation and the purpose to be achieved. At the same time, it also pointed out the nominal shortcomings of the proposed text. The Director of the Agricultural Forestry Chamber of Slovenia stressed that the proposed solutions stem from the action plan for young farmers and are similar to the solutions proposed by the Chamber. However, given the debate on land policy reform relating to agricultural land transport, it is reasonable to wait for comprehensive and systemic solutions in this area. Therefore, the House does not support the proposed legislative text. The representative of the Community of the municipalities of Slovenia pointed out that it would be necessary to include local communities on the list of pre-purchase beneficiaries for the purchase of land. With the right to pre-purchase agricultural land, local communities would acquire land where they would be more controlled to guide the development of all activities, in line with current needs, reduce land speculation and enable farmers to realistically relocate sealed farms to the margins of settlements or to open spaces where this would be acceptable from other aspects. In the context of the debate on individual articles, I, as Chairman of the Committee, on the basis of the rejection opinion of the Government and the comments of the Legislative and Legal Service, invited the Committee to discuss and decide, in accordance with the second paragraph of Article 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, a proposal for a decision that the proposal for a law is not suitable for further consideration, so that the proposer and the sending groups are given the opportunity to present their views at the meeting of the National Assembly and that the law is also voted on. After a brief discussion in which some members of the Committee supported the Community proposal of the municipalities of Slovenia on the necessity of including local communities on the list of pre-purchase beneficiaries in the purchase of agricultural land, the Committee agreed that the proposal for a law amending the Law on Agricultural Lands was not suitable for further consideration.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u187	Thank you for your word. Member Andrej Čuš proposes to amend the first paragraph of Article 17a of the Agricultural Land Act in such a way as to enable the owner to conclude a gift contract, the subject of which is agricultural land, forests or farm, with the holder of a farm which is the holder of a farm in accordance with the law governing agriculture and which is a young farmer in accordance with the Regulation on the Register of Farms or has acquired funds as a young farmer if it has not been more than 5 years since the acquisition of the farm. In this regard, I would like to clarify that the Government is familiar with the issue of transport and the purchase of agricultural land and has already approached a comprehensive reform of the area of agricultural land policy and relevant legislation. With a view to preparing professional starting points for a comprehensive renovation of the agricultural policy area and at a later stage also for the preparation of baselines for the amendment of the Agricultural Land Act, a professional basis for the re-regulation of agricultural policy was developed in February of this year, following the request of the Ministry of Agriculture, Forestry and Food, prepared by a group of experts from various Slovenian agricultural, higher education and other research institutions. This sets out three possible models of legal regulation for agricultural policy and proposes solutions, both in terms of agricultural land transport and in terms of the rules for the lease of protected farms and in the management of state land. The technical basis was first made public and then forwarded to the public on 24 February, with a deadline for comments by 30 June of this year. Five regional discussions will also be organised and, on the basis of the conclusions of these discussions, will be followed by the drafting of amendments to the relevant legislation. In view of the above, the Government therefore does not support amendments to the law that only partially address the area of agricultural land transport in one segment, and considers that a comprehensive approach should be taken to address the agricultural policy issue. In view of the above, it also considers that the law is not suitable for further consideration. Thank you.

The Director of the Fund for Agricultural Lands and Forests of the Republic of Slovenia, in the debate on the proposed provision of Article 17b of the draft law, explained that the Fund was already in its internal acts, i.e. in the development strategy and in the leasing rules, taking into account that priority should be given to young farmers or farmers. Thus, on the conditions laid down by law in the case of equivalent beneficiaries, the Fund ranks as a priority owner of an agricultural holding which is less than 40 years old and has acquired funding from the Rural Development Programme as a young acquirer. The second place, however, includes a young acquirer or agricultural operator, who is also under 40 years of age but has not run for funds. However, both are considered to have an advantage if they have less than 20 hectares of land to purchase. In the context of the debate on individual articles, as President of the Committee on the basis of the rejection of the opinion of the Government and the comments of the Legislative and Legal Service, the Committee proposed that, in accordance with the Rules of Procedure, it should discuss and decide on a proposal for a decision that the proposal for a law would not be suitable for further consideration, so that both the proposer and us parliamentary groups would be able to present their views on the draft law, as well as that the law should be voted on. We also expressed our conviction that the Fund is doing well, but it is not good legislation that governs its functioning. We have therefore called on the Government to prepare a new system law as soon as possible to deal with the issue of priority rights in the purchase of agricultural land, taking into account the interest of all stakeholders, including local communities, in the re-regulation of agricultural policy. Following the conclusion of the discussion, the Committee agreed that the proposal for a Law amending the Law on the Fund for Agricultural Lands and Forests of the Republic of Slovenia was not suitable for further consideration. ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u209	Dear President! Dear Members and Members! The Government has adopted a proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Housing Law. A reduced procedure is proposed for the consideration of the draft law as it concerns amendments and additions to the law relating to a decision of the Constitutional Court which found that Article 28 of the Law on the exercise of rights from public funds is inconsistent with the constitutional principle of equality. In addition, there are fewer difficult changes and amendments to the law. In view of the Constitutional Court’s decision that, with regard to the amount of the subsidy, the tenants are non-profitable and the tenants of commercial and household apartments in different situations, it is proposed to amend Article 121.b, which lays down general conditions for obtaining a subsidy for payment of the market rent. With a view to equating the situation of all beneficiaries of the subsidy, it is proposed that the beneficiaries of the subsidy be granted a non-profit share in addition to the market share of the subsidy. The new law also provides more clearly that applicants who pay market rent and have not only applied for a public tender are entitled to a subsidy for the payment of market rent, but have also been included on a priority list on the last public tender for the award of a non-profit flat for rent. It is proposed that the subsidy shall be paid at the time of payment of the market rent by the competent authority of the municipality of permanent residence irrespective of the municipality in which the applicant has a commercial residence and a fixed temporary residence. In this way, municipalities will be stimulated to receive housing programmes and to provide non-profit rentals. It is also very important that a provision allowing all tenants of non-profit dwellings, except tenants who have entered into a lease prior to the entry into force of the Housing Law, i.e. former tenants of housing rights, is required to provide evidence every 5 years of compliance with the general conditions for obtaining a non-profit lease. The proposal for amendments and the amendment of the Housing Law introduces a provision which would allow municipalities, public housing funds and non-profit housing organisations to obtain and process personal data from official records and personal data databases that are considered tax confidentiality and sensitive personal data that are essential for the performance of tasks in the housing field. In view of the need to promote greater activity of housing funds, additional debt is also proposed. The proposal of this Act gives the possibility that the Housing Fund of the Republic of Slovenia, in addition to the 10% of the debt already granted by the Public Funds Act, will be liable to an additional 20% of the declared assets of the Fund without limitation. In addition to this increased level of indebtedness, the amendments and additions to the Housing Law also make it possible for public housing funds to be additionally indebted to the performance of residential tasks. This follows the objective of increasing the non-profit housing unit fund, which is in the public interest. An additional debt of 10 % of the declared earmarked assets of the public fund, namely the Housing Fund of the Republic of Slovenia, is proposed, as well as a gradual debt of 2 % per year. Thank you.

In the debate, MEPs and Members supported the provisions of the bill following the Constitutional Court's decision, which concluded that Article 28 of the law was inconsistent with the constitutional principle of equality. In this regard, the State Secretary replied that there is also a problem in the opposite direction because there are some municipalities with a higher influx of temporary residents. From this point of view, it would be unfair for these municipalities to be burdened further, so the proposed decision of the bill is aimed at encouraging municipalities to provide more non-profit dwellings themselves. With regard to a notice from the Legislative and Legal Service that it would be appropriate to consider that the obligation to pay subsidies to its citizens, irrespective of the municipality in which the applicant has a commercial apartment, is imposed on the municipalities only if the applicant does not receive an adequate market apartment in that municipality, she stressed that it is very difficult or virtually impossible to determine how much rent is available in the individual municipality. In addition to the proposed provisions in the draft law, it was also pointed out in the debate that there are several other problems in the area of housing policy that the proposal does not solve. Among other things, some cases of irregularities were noted, which were to be used by multi-housing building managers in relation to the floor owners. The view was also expressed that a change in the value of the point to determine the non-profit rent would have to be faced sooner or later. The Secretary of State stressed that, in addition to other content, the issue will be addressed comprehensively in the proposal for a new law, which is expected to be ready by the end of this year. Following the discussion, the Committee adopted the amendment of the NSI Group to Article 2. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted on and adopted all the articles of the bill together. Thank you very much.

In view of the Constitutional Court’s decision that, with regard to the amount of the subsidy, the tenants are non-profit and rentable commercial and household apartments in different situations, it is proposed to amend Article 121.b, which lays down general conditions for obtaining a subsidy to payment of the market rent. It is proposed that beneficiaries be granted a non-profit share of the subsidy in addition to the market share of the subsidy. The new law also clearly provides that applicants who pay market rent and have not only applied for a public tender are entitled to a subsidy for the payment of market rents, but have also been included on a priority list on the last tender for the award of a non-profit flat for rent. It is proposed to abolish the requirement that the applicant be required to have a tenant of a market apartment in a municipality where he asks for a subsidy, permanent or at least temporary residence, or that the Centre for Social Work rejects the application for a subsidy. The current Housing Law does not provide an adequate legal basis for the acquisition and processing of personal data for the purpose of deciding and carrying out tasks in the residential area. It is also proposed that both the Republic and the municipal housing funds be additionally indebted. With the financial support of municipalities and additional financial obligations, it is difficult to talk about promoting the construction of non-profit dwellings, since the municipalities do not have enough resources to carry out legal tasks, let alone build housing. The bill brings some improvements that will show legal value in the implementation practice. We will not oppose the law in the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian Democratic Party.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u222	Mr Marjan Dolinšek will present the position of the Group of the Party of the Modern Centre. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u223	Honourable Presidents, Honourable Representatives of the Government, Honourable Members, Members of Parliament! The novelty of the Housing Law, which we are considering, brings about changes and additions relating to the Constitutional Court's decision, which points to the inequality before the law in the enforcement of rights from public funds, thus to the more precise contradictions found in the field of subsidies, non-profit and market rents. The Constitutional Court has found that there is an inequality before the law in how to determine the amount of the subsidy for tenants in non-profit apartments and tenants in commercial and household apartments that meet the income and other conditions for the acquisition of a non-profit apartment. The main solution to the proposal removes this inequality and equates the conditions for both groups of tenants. Equalization of all non-profit housing tenants is also an important contribution to the bill. It happens that some tenants do not actually meet the prescribed conditions for the acquisition of non-profit rental apartments, but, according to the existing legal provision, their status as tenant is virtually inviolable and that they no longer meet the criteria for the allocation of non-profit housing does not constitute a ground of guilt for the cancellation of the lease. The amendment of the law is equivalent in terms of the principle of equality for all tenants in non-profit apartments, irrespective of when the rental relationship has been concluded. The exceptions are the tenants of former tenants of housing rights who did not buy the apartment in which they were entitled and the former tenants of housing rights who remained in non-profit rentals in denationalised apartments. The existing practice has shown that there is no adequate legal basis in the current Housing Law for the acquisition and processing of personal data from official records for the purpose of deciding and carrying out tasks in the housing field. So it happens that in the records there is irregular or simply outdated data. The proposal removes this irregularity by allowing municipalities, public housing funds or non-profit housing organisations to obtain and process personal data on tenants and other participants in the procedure under the Housing Law and the regulations. In this way, in addition to the credibility of the data, it also affects the reduction of the burden on applicants who, in practice, had to obtain this information for themselves and their family members and to submit it to the competent authority themselves. And the last. In view of the lack of public rentals, the law also allows for an increase in the level of indebtedness. The possibility is therefore opened for the Housing Fund of the Republic of Slovenia, in addition to 10% of its debt, which is already available under the Public Funds Act, to be charged with an additional 20% of its declared earmarked assets without limitation and with the municipal housing fund for 10% of the dedicated assets of the municipal public fund, which can only be owed to the Housing Fund of the Republic of Slovenia. We believe that the bill adequately eliminates the anti-constitution found and can have a beneficial impact on the real estate market, both at national and municipal level, so we will support the proposal of the Modern Centre Party Group.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u237	Once again a nice greeting to all present! The proposer proposes to adopt a proposal for a new Law on National Housing Savings Schemes and Grants to Young Families for the First Resolution of the Housing Problem in a way that reintroduces the national housing savings scheme and subsidies to young families for the first resolution of the housing issue and rental subsidies. We already know such a solution from the past, as an identical arrangement was repealed by the Public Finance Balance Act, so-called ZUJF, in 2012. This fully deleted the articles relating to the granting of subsidies to young families and the subsidisation of commercial rental of dwellings. Even though the proposal of the law at first sight brings the solutions we like, we cannot ignore the fact that such solutions have not proved to be the most effective in the past. In particular, the negative effects of the austerity scheme have been shown to alter the terms of the savings and premiums, resulting in a lower interest in entering this austerity scheme, not adapting interest conditions to the current situation in the financial markets and facilitating the rental of loans and high non-refundable costs for the Housing Fund of the Republic of Slovenia as an operator of the National Housing Savings Scheme and the payer of subsidies. In view of past negative experience with the system that the applicant wishes to reintroduce, new, different orientations have been established in the area of housing policy. Finally, in this mandate on 24 November 2015, we adopted a Resolution on the National Housing Programme 2015-2025, which sets out the guidelines for housing policy, by which the State contributes to the achievement of the guidelines and objectives in the housing sector, which, in the first place, seek to ensure long-term quality of living for all people and also define in their individual parts the areas of young population groups. The resolution on the national housing programme during the first phase of the bibian self-reliance in young people is primarily encouraged by rent. The measures therefore relate to increasing the accessibility of the rental housing fund. The National Housing Programme plans to regulate the rental relationship and to establish a new rent policy to increase the supply of rental apartments from the existing housing fund; on the other hand, the new building measures focus on the provision of public rental apartments in larger employment centres where the need for housing is greatest. Thus, according to the existing system, we know different subsidies for rents, but they are naturally linked to certain income conditions, with families with fewer employees, young and young families specifically listed as a priority category. Today, we already know subsidies for non-profit and market rents, which account for up to 80% of the non-profit rent on the basis of the tenant's entitlement. The Ministry of Environment and Space also reports on activities in the area of housing problems for young and young families, where alternative solutions such as affordable housing for young and residential cooperatives are examined through pilot projects. For these reasons, and especially because the proposed solution in the past has not yielded the expected results, we believe that the proposal for a law is not suitable for further consideration in the Group of the Modern Centre Party.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u239	Dear, Vice President, Your Honor, colleagues and colleagues! The new law reintroduces the national housing savings scheme and subsidies to young families for the first resolution of the housing issue and rental subsidies. The proposal provides, inter alia, for the termination of Articles 207 and 208 of the Public Finance Balance Act, which are irrelevant to the re-introduction of the housing savings scheme and subsidies to young families for the first settlement of the housing issue. The possibility of entering into new savings contracts in the national housing savings scheme is introduced. The motion for a new law justifies the amendment by arguing that the problem of resolving the housing issue in young families is even more difficult. Young people, when in many cases they also support their parents or old parents, cannot save their own money to buy apartments. Moreover, because of unemployment or on-the-spot forms of employment, they cannot rent housing loans, while business banks also require non-life insurance for loans and marriages, and they reject the possibility of hiring loans even with an argument of excessive numbers of children. For this reason, it is necessary to reintroduce the possibility of saving and renting more favourable loans under the housing savings scheme. In the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian Democratic Party, we will support the bill.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u271	Honorable President, colleagues and colleagues! Triglav National Park is one of the oldest parks in Europe and the only national park in Slovenia. The main objective of the National Park is to protect nature, preserve exceptional natural and cultural values, rare and endangered plant and animal species, and preserve and nurture cultural landscapes. Nevertheless, it must be recognized that since the establishment of the park there have been residents who have been trying to coexist with the Triglav National Park. As regards the protected area of the Triglav National Park, it is noted that during the last period the Municipality of Bohinj and the inhabitants of this area have been intensely alerted to the unsustainable coexistence in Triglav National Park. This is also affected by the harmonisation of Slovenian environmental legislation with European legislation. Many initiatives have highlighted the difficulty of staying within the Triglav National Park, and thus the growing indisposition towards or rejection of the protected area is becoming more common. In 2010, the Law on the Triglav National Park was adopted, detailing all the prohibitions, restrictions and financial incentives that should have helped the inhabitants of the area to stay in the protected area. Since the Law on the Triglav National Park is only implemented in part of the restriction, the prohibition, we have proposed two recommendations to facilitate the implementation of the law in the New Slovenia Group of Christians together with unrelated Members of the Government. At the meeting of the Committee on Infrastructure, Environment and Space, a recommendation was adopted following a heated debate: The National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia recommends that the Government of the Republic of Slovenia provide the budget with adequate funds for the implementation of Articles 10 and 11 of the Law on the Triglav National Park. It was accepted by a large majority. This is a step towards more effective implementation of the Triglav National Park Act. Members and Members of the New Slovenia Group – Christian Democrats will be accepted unanimously.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u275	Honourable President of the National Assembly, Honourable Secretary of State with a co-worker, Member of Parliament and Members of the National Assembly! At its 28th meeting, the Committee on Infrastructure, Environment and Space considered a proposal for a recommendation on the effective implementation of the Law on the Triglav National Park, submitted to the National Assembly by a group of Members and Members with first-signed mags. Matthew Tonin. An additional explanation on behalf of the applicants was submitted by the MEP. Alenka Bratušek pointed out that the establishment of a protected or protected area, such as the National Park, for municipalities and citizens represents several types of restrictions on living in such an area. These restrictions are contained in Article 10 of the Law on the Triglav National Park, which sets out development guidelines that ensure the achievement of environmental protection objectives and promote sustainable development, adapted to the objectives of the Triglav National Park. In view of the limitations that municipalities and citizens face in the Triglav National Park area, Article 11 provides that additional funding for projects, investments and activities carried out by park local communities in the national park shall be provided by the park local community from the State budget for co-financing of 80 % at the estimated cost of the project or investment or the means necessary to carry out the activities, unless the other provision provides for a higher percentage of funding. The opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service on the proposal for a recommendation was presented by Helena Rus. She pointed out that the Legislative and Legal Service had mainly pointed to point 1 of the proposal for a recommendation that was substantively open to this extent, that it was not possible to understand which measures to facilitate the implementation of the law were proposed and that they had also made an inappropriate reference in the preamble to the proposal for a recommendation, which was duly taken into account in the amendment of the NSI Group. On behalf of the Government, the Secretary of State, Mrs Lidija Stebernak, stressed that the law provides for the establishment and management of the Triglav National Park as a priority for the conservation of nature and that, in its area, coordinated development is possible with natural and created data. Promoting sustainable development is specified in Articles 10 and 11, the method of financing under Articles 55 and 56. In addition to the law, several other documents have been incorporated since 2014, the most important of which is the Management Plan Regulation of the Triglav National Park for the period 2016 to 2025. With regard to the budget allocated for the implementation of the law, she stressed that, in the period 2010-2016, an average of EUR 450 000 had been paid in the public service, with an estimated EUR 585 000 in 2017, an estimated EUR 89,5 million had been planned in order to carry out the tasks for the ten-year period and the public administration’s own resources of approximately EUR 2.5 million. The parishes of Bohinj, Tolmin, Kranjska Gora highlighted the problems that the municipalities face within the Triglav National Park in relation to the implementation of the law. They pointed out that since the adoption of the law no provisions have been implemented providing that 80 % of the budget of the project for projects carried out by the local park communities are provided from the State budget. In the debate, the opposition Member and Members supported the demands of the municipalities and pointed out that one of the main reasons for the amendment until then was the law in force, the adoption of the law in that the current law mainly contained prohibitions and restrictions on environmental interventions within the Triglav National Park, and the law also sought to legislate dictions which, despite restrictions, allowed for normal development in this area. With regard to the debates between Members and Members, the State Secretary and the representative of the Environment Directorate explained that about €7 million is earmarked for the type of nature in the budget at annual level, of which 20% is intended for the implementation of the law. In the Triglav National Park area, a number of projects have been carried out so far, which are always partly related not only to nature but also to developmental content. Following the discussion, the Committee adopted the amendment of the NSI Group's amendment to the preamble, and the Committee also adopted two of its amendments so as to keep the part of the second point of the recommendation supplemented. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u277	Thank you for your word, Madam President. Members and Members, a key document for the effective management of the National Park for the implementation of the Law on the Triglav National Park is the 10-year management plan of the Triglav National Park, which was adopted in May 2016. At the Ministry of Environment and Space, we believe that the governance plan addresses, in addition to defining the protection objectives, all the important areas covered by Article 10 of the Law on the Triglav National Park, namely the development guidelines on agriculture and forestry, the promotion of economic, social, social and environmental development towards the national park, friendly tourism and activities that contribute to the preservation of jobs, the protection of nature, the preservation of cultural heritage, sustainable mobility and the use of energy. It is important to stress that the Ministry of Environment and Space invests a great deal of energy and resources in the implementation of this plan. Good results in the area of development and nature protection in the national park can only be expected if we manage to connect all parties and stakeholders with an integrated approach, in addition to the Ministry of Environment and Space and the Public Institute, the Triglav National Park also ministries concerned with the above areas, as well as local environments with municipalities, development agencies, local tourism organisations and other institutions. The management plan provides for €89.5 million for the 10-year management of the National Park. The Ministry of the Environment and Space for the implementation of the TNP management plan during this period will allocate €23 million from the state budget and nearly €64 million from the ministries. On the basis of the governance plan in 2017, almost € 1.6 million is allocated to the Public Institute of TNP and an additional €210 000 will be allocated for the implementation of the development tasks in 2018. Let me stress that the financing of the Triglav National Park currently stands for the Ministry of the Environment and Space for one fifth of all the resources allocated by the Ministry to preserve nature. With regard to the implementation of Article 11 of the Law on the Triglav National Park, which provides that, in connection with the development guidelines referred to in Article 10, park local communities are provided with additional funds of 80 % of the budget value and that an additional 10 % of the points are provided for in the tenders, certain problems have been identified by the ministries. As a result of this Article, the provision on the allocation of an additional 10% of the points in calls for proposals for projects applying for the area of park communities or for the area of the national park is largely implemented. The 80 % co-financing requirement for projects in recent years, which meant a restrictive period for appropriations from the budget, has at least partially been achieved by applying for projects from various European funds, in particular energy recovery projects, municipal infrastructure, road infrastructure, employment and scholarships. Projects currently being implemented, which also relate to the implementation of Articles 10 and 11 of the Law, totalling around EUR 10 million. The Public Institute of Triglav National Park cooperates closely with local communities, local tourism organisations, regional development agencies and other local institutions in the implementation of various projects with very broad content. In recent years, some €2.5 million has also come into the area in this way, so we are confident that the implementation of the governance plan can achieve positive effects in the area and thus contribute to the development of a sustainable economy of local areas in the Triglav National Park and at the same time to the preservation of nature. Thank you.

It must also provide for a stable management of the Public Institute of Triglav National Park. Since 2013, there has been no Director with full powers in this institution. Since 2013, we have a temporary director who has been extended from year to year, and as soon as it appears, until the full mandate. ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u284	Mr Igor Zorčič, of the Modern Centre Party Group, has the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u285	Mr President, thank you for saying so. Dear representatives of the government, colleagues, colleagues! The Triglav National Park is a Slovenian national pearl, has special status, is the only national park in Slovenia and has a special nature conservation regime, which is stricter than in landscape parks. Such a regime is aimed in particular at preserving animal and plant species, ecosystems, as well as parts of the park, and at preserving cultural heritage and landscape. The unique part of the alpine mountain reflects the long-term coexistence of the inhabitants and nature there for many years. We must ensure that this part of the unspoiled nature is adequately protected in the future, and that we allow sustainable development for the people. In May 2016, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia adopted the Management Plan of the Triglav National Park for the period 2016-2025, which sets out long-term and operational objectives, tasks and envisaged activities for the conservation of nature and natural resources, the preservation of cultural heritage and cultural landscape, the preservation of migration and sustainable development, visits and experiences and joint support activities for the effective implementation of all these areas. In addition to this plan, it also sets out as a very important segment the scope of the visit of the National Park, which commits to ensuring an adequate, regulated and maintained infrastructure and a supportive environment. It provides that a network of information points and park infrastructure will be upgraded to achieve the objectives set. Priority will be given to the management of the visit and activities in the field of information, awareness-raising and quality management of visitors will be strengthened. However, in practice, the great burden of protection and development, including with many restrictions, falls on park local communities. With a view to the financial recovery of these local communities, the Triglav National Park Act clearly provides that the projects, investments and activities carried out or provided by park local communities in the national park shall be provided with additional funds from the State budget for co-financing of 80 % of the estimated value of the project or investment or assets. The fact is that this provision has not so far been implemented under any government, and it is time for this to change. With the aim of preserving nature, landscape and cultural heritage, there has been and will also be a complex process in the future management of the Triglav National Park, which requires a consensus of stakeholders at the highest level, and in particular good governance with our only national park requires respect for the values and rules that we have set ourselves in the past. The Law on the Triglav National Park provides very clearly for money to carry out the tasks under this Act, and we will therefore recommend in the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre that they be provided in the budget, supported and, of course, the final decision on these funds will be taken by adopting each budget. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u287	Dear President, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! The Triglav National Park is the only national park, and as a result it is all the more important how legislation is implemented. The fact is that the establishment of a protected or protected area constitutes a restriction for the municipalities and people living there. The law in force was adopted in the National Assembly in 2010 by virtually unanimous agreement. The law brought great news and, in addition to nature’s limitations, brought articles that provided further incentives for the development of this protected area, both for a narrower part directly in the park and for park municipalities. Unfortunately, through the discussions in the National Assembly, we find that virtually all nature conservation measures are being implemented, but that the instruments for development initiatives and projects are very rare and are not felt by the communities and people in the park. The Triglav National Park Act is a comprehensive law and specifies exactly how things should be done. It also accurately defines how incentives and co-financing are allocated. From the adoption of the law until 2017, the municipalities received virtually no funds from this title. In the same period, Ljubljana received 87 million from the same state budget under the Special Law on the capital of the Republic of Slovenia, from 2010 to 2017. Why are the congregations within the Triglav National Park not at least equal to the capital city and not getting what is legally theirs? The government says that, due to the financial framework of the institution, the Triglav National Park has been declining but has not been reduced or implemented only the development part of the law, while restrictions and prohibitions have not been reduced. There have always been sufficient resources for all supervisory functions and bureaucracy. In the past, the National Assembly also agreed that the seat of the Public Institute of Triglav National Park should be transferred to the municipality which has the largest population in the park, and this is Bohinj, which has not yet been carried out. The promises that have been in the marriage for seven years have been the key thing that has led to such a large majority of marriages. These development incentives, as defined in Articles 10 and 11, are not yet being implemented or are being implemented to a very small extent and will have to be taken a step further by the Government and the Ministry of Environment and Space. Otherwise, our only national park, our jewel, with which we often like to boast at home and abroad, will be lost in the future. They will also lose municipalities because they are limited in development and lose their population because they are limited to a number of aspects of their business activities. This stepmother's relationship is also evident through the human resources problem, as we still do not have a director with full powers from 2013. The question is, Is it not intended that things should not be done as they should, and as stated in the law? The policy must gather sufficient wisdom to choose, however, from the tenders, someone who is competent and competent to carry out this work. This is also why there is considerable disappointment at the work of the Ministry and the Public Institute of the Triglav National Park, because since the promises made so far in this National Assembly, what needs to be changed, how to improve and implement the law in its entirety, obviously nothing changes. If nothing changes in a very short period of time, problems will only exacerbate and conflicts with the people in the park itself and with local communities will be able to question the existence of a park in the current range, so we support the recommendation of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia to provide in the budget adequate funds for the implementation of Articles 10 and 11 of the Law on the Triglav National Park.

The fact is that the establishment of a protected or protected area, such as the National Park, for municipalities and citizens represents several types of restrictions on the residence in such an area. Environmentalists, of course, claim that these restrictions are still too small, while the local population thinks they are too harsh. These restrictions, which are laid down in Article 10 of the Law, are those which are intended to ensure the achievement of the objectives of environmental protection while at the same time promoting sustainable development for the adapted objectives of the Triglav National Park. Due to the limitations that are naturally most felt by municipalities and citizens in the Triglav National Park area, Article 11 provides that additional co-financing resources shall be provided for projects, investments and activities within the Triglav National Park from the State budget. It is expected to be 80 % of the estimated value of the project or investment. This is the part where we meet with two different explanations. One by the Ministry and one by the municipalities. The municipalities located in the Triglav National Park area claim that no funds have been received from this title since the adoption of the law since 2010. Given that a rather good number of projects worth €18 million have been carried out in the Triglav National Park area, which have built or upgraded information centres, business and other infrastructure have been built, many activities, such as cleaning and management of the mountains, have apparently been maintained by the Ministry. This does not mean that the congregations are misleading, but more than evidently the municipalities have different criteria than the ministry as to what they are and what they are not state resources. In the area of Triglav National Park, projects are being implemented and co-financed by the State. Probably not as much as the congregations would have wanted. If we look at the projects carried out, the Triglav National Park is mainly co-financed by projects related to nature conservation, rather than projects that would make life easier for the inhabitants of Triglav National Park there and help to preserve the population of the area and which the municipalities would obviously want. In the Desus Group, we believe that the first ten-year management plan adopted by this government in cooperation with SOS and Gorenje regional development agencies will help to improve the financing of development projects. This is a central document covering the actions and tasks of all other ministries, not only environmental ones. The tasks of each ministry and public institution are also defined by the financial part that provides for €89 million for a decade, of which €60 is for sustainable development and preservation of population. The Desus Group will support the proposal for a recommendation, which was slightly modified at the meeting of the parent working body, but with it the National Assembly recommends that the Government provide funds in the budget for the implementation of Articles 10 and 11 of the Law on the Triglav National Park. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2017-05-23-SDZ7-Redna-30.ana.u307	Thank you for your word. Respected and respected! I would like to make a brief comment on this recommendation. I would say, however, that in 1981 the Law on the Triglav National Park was passed, which established the area of the only national park in Slovenia, which is actually a real Slovenian pearl. The law establishes a special regime that restricts the living population there and prescribes a special nature conservation regime within the Triglav National Park. This regime, of course, limits and prohibits certain interventions into space, which, of course, causes friction of the living population because there are different interests, and this is a problem. In the Triglav National Park in the last period the Municipality of Bohinj and the inhabitants of this area have been intensely pointing out the incoherence of cohabitation in Triglav National Park, including with a view to harmonising Slovenian environmental legislation with European legislation. Many initiatives highlight the difficulty of staying within the Triglav National Park, the indisposition and refusal to protect the area. The municipality of Bohinj points out that it is faced with the provisions of the law which are limited in the performance of legal duties and the inhabitants in everyday life, while also pointing out that the State does not implement the law, in particular Articles 10 and 11 of the Law on the Triglav National Park. It also states that in 2010 it continued to implement 60 projects, which were not co-financed by the Republic of Slovenia in the manner prescribed by the law. These were various projects, to quickly list them: maintenance of forest paths, cycling routes, other routes, including around the lake, measures aimed at preserving and developing agriculture and rural areas, regulating municipal infrastructure and so on. It would briefly touch on the Regulation on the Management Plan for the Triglav National Park for the period 2016 to 2025, which was adopted in 2016. This plan ensures the integrity of systemic solutions, means the obligation of all sources to take into account the objectives set in the Triglav National Park Act, namely environmental protection and nature protection and support for local development. Each task under the Triglav National Park Management Plan shall be subject to the necessary activities, operators and participating stakeholders. The implementation of the Law on the Triglav National Park is, of course, beyond the framework of the Ministry of Environment and Space and therefore the management plan also sets out the tasks and activities falling within the competence of other ministries. The main problem seen by mayors of areas within the Triglav National Park is that the State does not implement Article 11, which is co-financing projects so that, on the other hand, the obligations of municipalities and citizens within the Triglav National Park are not reduced. Of course that's the problem. The Ministry of Environment and Space said that the municipalities had received certain funds relating to nature conservation and other purposes. Ms Stebernak, State Secretary, also assured today that all the activities necessary to protect the nature and monuments in the Triglav National Park are being carried out and that the Ministry is working to ensure that these things are leaked. We're on the board. [[turn off the microphone]]
